{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove the perfect-matching entropy conjecture of Anari, Oveis Gharan, and Vinzant. For every feasible vector <i>x</i> of perfect-matching edge marginals in a loopless labelled multigraph on $`2m\\ge2`$ vertices, the maximum entropy $`H(x)`$ of a matching law with marginals <i>x</i> satisfies\n\n$`\\displaystyle F(x)-(2-2/m)B(x)\\le H(x)\\le F(x),`$\n\nwhere $`F(x)=-\\sum_e x_e\\log x_e`$ and $`B(x)=-\\sum_e(1-x_e)\\log(1-x_e)`$. This bound holds throughout the polytope, including its boundary. <a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the perfect-matching entropy conjecture of Anari, Oveis Gharan, and Vinzant. For every feasible vector <i>x</i> of perfect-matching edge marginals in a loopless labelled multigraph on $`2m\ge2`$ vertices, the maximum entropy $`H(x)`$ of a matching law with marginals <i>x</i> satisfies
> 
> $`\displaystyle F(x)-(2-2/m)B(x)\le H(x)\le F(x),`$
> 
> where $`F(x)=-\sum_e x_e\log x_e`$ and $`B(x)=-\sum_e(1-x_e)\log(1-x_e)`$. This bound holds throughout the polytope, including its boundary. We also prove the sharp bound $`|\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits x|-\dim F_x\le3m-2`$, where <i>F</i><sub><i>x</i></sub> is the minimal face of the perfect-matching polytope containing <i>x</i>.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ent-main)): Let $G$ be a loopless multigraph on $2m\ge2$ vertices with at least one perfect matching. For every $x\in P(G)$, $$\label{intro:main-bound} F(x)-\left(2-\frac2m\right)B(x) \le H(x)\le F(x).$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the maximum entropy of matching laws with a prescribed feasible mean by separable coordinate functions, including boundary means.
- **C2** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-thm-algorithm-guarantee)): **Theorem 2** (Deterministic approximate counting). *There is one uniform deterministic algorithm which, on a graph on $n$ vertices with nonnegative binary integer pair multiplicities, returns an integer $A\ge0$ satisfying $$N(G)/512^n\le A\le N(G).$$ Its running time and output length are polynomial in the input bit length. In particular, $A=0$ exactly when $N(G)=0$. The guarantee includes a compact input consisting of binary $n$ and a list of positive pair records.*
  - Explanation: Constructs a uniform deterministic approximation to the labelled perfect-matching count with exact zero recognition and polynomial bit complexity.
- **C3** (construction, [source](#stmt-a-lem-tight-cut-gluing)): **Lemma 3** (Gluing across an odd cut). *Suppose $y_i\in P(G_i)$, for $i=1,2$, and their coordinates agree on $C$. There is a point $y\in P(G)$ with the internal coordinates of the two $y_i$ and their common crossing coordinates.*
  - Explanation: Glues matching laws in contracted graphs when their labelled crossing-edge marginals agree.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-thm-polytope)): For every graph $G$ as above, $$\label{geom:eq-polytope} P(G)=\left\{x\in\mathbb R^E: \begin{aligned} x_e&\ge0 &&(e\in E),\\ x(\delta(v))&=1 &&(v\in V),\\ x(\delta(S))&\ge1 &&(S\subseteq V,\ \|S\|\text{ odd}) \end{aligned}\right\}.$$
  - Explanation: Characterizes feasible perfect-matching means by nonnegativity, unit vertex degrees and odd-cut constraints.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-lem-face)): For every $x\in P(G)$, the space $T(x)$ consists exactly of the vectors satisfying $$\label{geom:eq-directions} \begin{aligned} h_e&=0 &&\text{if }x_e=0,\\ h(\delta(v))&=0 &&(v\in V),\\ h(\delta(S))&=0 &&\text{if }\|S\|\text{ is odd and }x(\delta(S))=1. \end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $F_x$ is the minimal face containing $x$, and $$\label{geom:eq-face-span} \operatorname{aff}F_x=x+T(x),\qquad F_x=\operatorname{conv}\mathcal A_x,\qquad T(x)=\operatorname{span}\{a-x:a\in\mathcal A_x\}.$$ The point $x$ lies in the relative interior of $F_x$, and every law on perfect matching vectors with mean $x$ is supported on $\mathcal A_x$.
  - Explanation: Identifies the minimal face, its two-sided direction space and the matching atoms that can support a law with the prescribed mean.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-geom-prop-rank)): Let $\|V\|=2m\ge2$, and suppose $x\in P(G)$ has $x_e>0$ for every edge. Then $$\label{geom:eq-codimension} \|E\|-d(x)\le3m-2.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds face codimension on positive edge coordinates without requiring connectedness or strictly fractional means.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-lem-reduction)): Let $x\in P(G)$. The number of vertices in $V_f(x)$ is even; write $\|V_f(x)\|=2m_f$. Let $G_f=(V_f(x),E_f(x))$ and $\bar x=x\|_{E_f(x)}$. The coordinate extension $$\label{geom:eq-extension} \iota(z)_e= \begin{cases} z_e,&e\in E_f(x),\\ x_e,&e\notin E_f(x) \end{cases}$$ is an affine isomorphism from $P(G_f)$ onto the face $$Q_x=\{y\in P(G):y_e=x_e\text{ for }e\notin E_f(x)\}.$$ It sends the minimal face of $\bar x$ onto $F_x$. For every $z\in P(G_f)$, removing the fixed edges gives a bijection between laws with mean $\iota(z)$ and laws on perfect matchings of $G_f$ with mean $z$, preserving their probabilities. Moreover, $$\label{geom:eq-fractional-rank} \sum_{e\in E_f(x)}x_e=m_f,\qquad d(x)\ge \|E_f(x)\|-3m_f+2\quad\text{if }m_f\ge1.$$ If $m_f=0$, then $x$ is a matching vector and $d(x)=0$.
  - Explanation: Removes fixed coordinates through an affine face isomorphism and a probability-preserving bijection of matching laws.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ent-continuity)): For a nonempty perfect-matching polytope $P(G)$, the maximum mean entropy $H$ is attained, nonnegative, concave and continuous on the whole polytope.
  - Explanation: Establishes attainment, nonnegativity, concavity and continuity of maximum mean entropy on a nonempty matching polytope.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ent-exponential)): Every law with mean $x\in P(G)$ is supported on $\mathcal A_x$. Its entropy maximizer is positive on every atom of $\mathcal A_x$ and has the form $$\label{ent:gibbs} p_a=\frac{e^{\theta\cdot a}}{\sum_{b\in\mathcal A_x}e^{\theta\cdot b}} \quad(a\in\mathcal A_x)$$ for a finite vector $\theta\in\mathbb R^E$. For every $\eta\in\mathbb R^E$, the law obtained by replacing $\theta$ by $\eta$ maximizes entropy at its own mean, and that mean has minimal face $F_x$.
  - Explanation: Expresses the entropy-maximizing law as a positive exponential law on the atoms of its minimal face.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-ent-trace)): Let $G$ have $2m\ge2$ vertices, and suppose that $x\in P(G)$ has $0<x_e<1$ for every edge. If $J_c-H$ has a local maximum at $x$, then, with $d=\dim F_x$ and $s=\|E\|$, $$\label{ent:trace-obstruction} 0\ge d-s+(c+1)m.$$
  - Explanation: Derives a covariance trace obstruction at a local maximum of a separable entropy deficit with strictly fractional coordinates.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ent-components)): For $x\in P(G)$, remove its zero edges and the endpoints of its value-one edges. Let the nonempty connected components of the remaining graph have $2m_i$ vertices, and let $B_i$ denote the sum defining $B$ on component $i$. Then $$F(x)-\sum_i(2-2/m_i)B_i(x)\le H(x)\le F(x).$$ If there are no remaining components, $F(x)=B(x)=H(x)=0$.
  - Explanation: Refines the entropy comparison by applying it separately to the connected fractional components.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-cons-defect)): For every $x\in P$, $$\label{cons:defect-bound} 0\le F(x)-H(x) \le \min\{F(x),\,2m-2\}.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the entropy defect by both the marginal entropy and an order-dependent additive error.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-cons-entropy-count)): One has $$\begin{aligned} \ln N&\le\max_{x\in P}F(x)\le\ln N+2m-2, \label{cons:F-count}\\ \ln N&\le\max_{x\in P}H_{\mathrm{bin}}(x) \le\ln N+3m-2. \label{cons:binary-count} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Estimates the logarithm of the matching count by maxima of separable marginal entropy and binary-coordinate entropy.
- **C14** (bound, [source](#stmt-cons-structural)): Let $G$ be a finite loopless multigraph on $2m>0$ vertices that contains $k\ge1$ pairwise edge-disjoint perfect matchings. If $k=1$, then $N(G)\ge1$. If $k\ge2$, then $$\label{cons:finite-order-count} N(G)\ge k^m(1-1/k)^{2(m-1)(k-1)} \ge e^{-2(m-1)}k^m \ge(e^{-2}k)^m.$$
  - Explanation: Gives explicit count lower bounds from a selected family of pairwise edge-disjoint perfect matchings.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cons-regular)): Let $G$ be a finite loopless $k$-regular multigraph on $2m>0$ vertices, where $k\ge1$. Suppose $\lvert\delta(S)\rvert\ge k$ for every odd vertex set $S$. Then the conclusions of Theorem <a href="#cons:structural" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cons:structural">14</a> hold. In particular, they hold for every $(k-1)$-edge-connected $k$-regular graph of positive even order.
  - Explanation: Transfers the structural lower counts to regular graphs whose odd cuts satisfy the stated degree condition.
- **C16** (construction, [source](#stmt-ex-lem-expansion-face)): The set $$Q_T=\{y\in P(G'):y(\delta(T))=1\}$$ is a face of $P(G')$ affinely isomorphic to $P(G)$. Under this isomorphism an old edge keeps its coordinate and $$y_{f_i}=y_{e_i}\qquad(1\le i\le3).$$ If $x\in P(G)$ has coordinate $1/3$ on every edge, its image $x'$ has coordinate $1/3$ on every edge of $G'$. The isomorphism maps the minimal face at $x$ onto the minimal face at $x'$.
  - Explanation: Identifies the cut-tight face created by a triangle expansion with the original matching polytope.
- **C17** (construction, [source](#stmt-ex-prop-klee-face)): For every integer $m\ge1$, the graph $G_m$ and the point $x^{(m)}$ above satisfy $$F_{x^{(m)}}= \operatorname{conv}\{a(M_1),a(M_2),a(M_3)\}, \qquad d(x^{(m)})=2.$$ The unique law on perfect matchings with mean $x^{(m)}$ is the uniform law on $M_1,M_2,M_3$. Consequently, $$\label{ex:eq-klee-values} \begin{aligned} \|E(G_m)\|-d(x^{(m)})&=3m-2,\\ H(x^{(m)})&=\ln3,\\ F(x^{(m)})&=m\ln3,\\ B(x^{(m)})&=2m\ln(3/2). \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Constructs uniform means with a triangular minimal face, unique matching law and sharp supported face codimension.
- **C18** (bound, [source](#stmt-ex-cor-coefficient)): If a constant $c$ satisfies $$H(x)\ge F(x)-cB(x)$$ for every finite loopless multigraph $G$ and every $x\in P(G)$, then $$c\ge\frac{\ln3}{2\ln(3/2)}.$$ The same necessary bound holds if the assertion is restricted to simple graphs.
  - Explanation: Uses the triangle-expansion family to give a necessary lower bound on any universal entropy comparison coefficient, also for simple graphs.
- **C19** (bound, [source](#stmt-cons-weighted)): For every finite real vector $\beta$, $$\label{cons:weighted-bracket} q^*-\frac{2m-2}{\ln2} \le\log_2 Z_\beta\le q^*.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the binary logarithm of a weighted partition function by a separable entropy optimum and an additive correction.
- **C20** (bound, [source](#stmt-d-coarse-weighted)): For every finite loopless labelled multigraph on an even number $n\ge2$ of vertices with at least one perfect matching, and every finite real vector $\beta\in\mathbb R^E$, $$\label{d:eq-coarse-weighted} q^*-4n\le\log_2 Z_\beta\le q^*+n.$$
  - Explanation: Provides a conservative weighted bracket for subsequent rational optimization and rounding.
- **C21** (bound, [source](#stmt-d-lem-penalized-count)): The contribution of matchings using an absent pair satisfies $$0\le Z_{\mathrm{bad}}\le2^{-20n}.$$ Moreover, $$\label{d:eq-penalized-count} \begin{aligned} N=0&\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \log_2 Z_\beta\le-20n,\\ N>0&\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \max\{1,2^{-n/2}N\}\le Z_\beta\le2N. \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Controls the contribution of absent pairs after weighted completion and separates zero from positive input counts.
- **C22** (bound, [source](#stmt-d-lem-dyadic-entropy)): The function $\psi$ is concave, with integer slopes of absolute value at most $K$. For every $x\in P$, $$\label{d:eq-dyadic-entropy-error} 0\le h_2(x)-\sum_e\psi(x_e) \le\frac n2+1\le n.$$
  - Explanation: Approximates binary marginal entropy by a concave dyadic interpolant with integral slopes and a controlled additive error.
- **C23** (construction, [source](#stmt-d-lem-feasibility-repair)): Set $A_0=10n^2$. For every $x\in[0,1]^p$, there is $y\in P$ such that $$\\|y-x\\|_\infty\le (2+A_0)w(x).$$
  - Explanation: Repairs an arbitrary box point to a feasible complete-graph matching mean with distance controlled by its maximum constraint violation.
- **C24** (theorem, [source](#stmt-d-lem-exact-penalty)): The function $\Phi$ is concave on $[0,1]^p$, and $$\max_{x\in[0,1]^p}\Phi(x)=f^*.$$ At every point of the box, $\Phi$ has an integral supergradient $g$ satisfying $\\|g\\|_2^2\le D_0$.
  - Explanation: Turns the constrained rational objective into an exact concave penalty objective with bounded integral supergradients.
- **C25** (bound, [source](#stmt-d-lem-projected-ascent)): Let $N_{\mathrm{it}}=pD_0$, set $x^{(0)}=0$, and for $j=0,\ldots,N_{\mathrm{it}}-1$ choose a supergradient $g^{(j)}$ as in Lemma <a href="#d:lem-exact-penalty" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="d:lem-exact-penalty">24</a> and set $$\label{d:eq-projected-iterations} x^{(j+1)} =\Pi_{[0,1]^p}\left(x^{(j)}+\frac{g^{(j)}}{D_0}\right),$$ where $\Pi_{[0,1]^p}$ clips each coordinate to $[0,1]$. Then $$\label{d:eq-optimization-accuracy} R=\left\lfloor\max_{0\le j<N_{\mathrm{it}}}\Phi(x^{(j)})\right\rfloor \qquad\text{satisfies}\qquad R\le f^*\le R+2.$$
  - Explanation: Gives a finite projected-ascent schedule whose largest trial objective determines an integer approximation to the constrained optimum.
- **C26** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-d-lem-unit-mincut)): For distinct $a,b\in W$, a minimum cut separating $a$ from $b$ can be found deterministically using at most $C$ unit augmentations and $O(v^2(C+1))$ arithmetic operations on integers of $O(\log_2(v+C+2))$ bits.
  - Explanation: Computes a deterministic two-terminal minimum cut using integral unit augmentations with explicit arithmetic bounds.
- **C27** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-d-lem-odd-cut-oracle)): Let $U\subseteq W$ have positive even cardinality. One can deterministically find a set $S\subseteq W$ minimizing $c(S)$ subject to $\|S\cap U\|$ being odd. The algorithm returns the set itself, uses at most $\|U\|-1$ recursive instances, and requires $O(\|U\|^3v^2(C+1))$ arithmetic operations on integers of $O(\log_2(v+C+2))$ bits.
  - Explanation: Computes a minimum odd-terminal cut by parity-preserving contraction and returns its lifted vertex set.
- **C28** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-d-prop-bit-complexity)): The algorithm above is uniform and deterministic, and its running time and output length are polynomial in the input bit length.
  - Explanation: Proves polynomial bit complexity and polynomial output length for the uniform deterministic counting construction.
- **C29** (construction, [source](#stmt-geom-lem-laminar)): Suppose $x\in P(G)$ and $x_e>0$ for every edge. There is a laminar family $\mathcal L\subseteq\mathcal S_x$ whose cut vectors are linearly independent and span all the cut vectors $\chi_S$, $S\in\mathcal S_x$.
  - Explanation: Spans tight odd-cut rows by an independent laminar family at a positive feasible mean.
- **C30** (bound, [source](#stmt-a-prop-supported-codimension)): For a loopless labelled multigraph on $n=2m\ge2$ vertices and $x\in P(G)$, put $A=\{e:x_e>0\}$ and $s_A=\|A\|$. Then $s_A-d(x)\le3m-2$. In particular, $$\label{a:eq-full-support-codimension} s_A-d(x)\le 2(n-2)\quad(n\ge4), \qquad s_A-d(x)=1\quad(n=2).$$ Hence $s_A-d(x)\le2n=4m$. For the empty graph, $P(G)$ is a singleton in zero coordinates and the codimension is zero.
  - Explanation: Bounds supported face codimension, with separate elementary-order and empty-graph cases.
- **C31** (bound, [source](#stmt-a-cor-fractional-dimension)): For every $x\in P(G)$, the integer $s_f$ is even, and $$\label{a:eq-fractional-dimension} d(x)\ge \begin{cases} \|E_f(x)\|-2(s_f-2),&s_f\ge4,\\ \|E_f(x)\|-1,&s_f=2,\\ 0,&s_f=0, \end{cases} \qquad \sum_{e\in E_f(x)}x_e=\frac{s_f}{2}.$$
  - Explanation: Isolates the even fractional vertex set and derives residual face-dimension lower bounds.
- **C32** (theorem, [source](#stmt-a-thm-separable)): Let $G$ be a finite loopless labelled multigraph of even order $n=2m$, with a nonempty perfect-matching family. With natural logarithms and continuous endpoint conventions, define $$J_c(x)=F(x)-cB(x) =\sum_{e\in E}\bigl[-x_e\ln x_e+c(1-x_e)\ln(1-x_e)\bigr].$$ For each $c\in\{3,4\}$ and every $x\in P(G)$, $$\label{a:eq-separable-bounds} J_c(x)\le H(x)\le F(x)+B(x).$$ For $n=0$, all displayed quantities are zero.
  - Explanation: Proves separable entropy comparisons from coarser face-dimension estimates and covariance second variations.
- **C33** (bound, [source](#stmt-cons-legacy-defect)): For every $x\in P(G)$ with $\|V(G)\|=n=2m>0$, $$\begin{aligned} F(x)-4B(x)&\le F(x)-3B(x)\le H(x)\le F(x)+B(x),\label{cons:legacy-separable}\\ F(x)-H(x)&\le\min\{F(x),3m\} \le8m\bigl(1-e^{-F(x)/m}\bigr).\label{cons:legacy-nonlinear} \end{aligned}$$ Furthermore, $$\label{cons:legacy-bin-gap} \ln N(G)\le\max_{x\in P(G)}[F(x)+B(x)] \le\ln N(G)+2n \le\ln N(G)+\tfrac52n.$$
  - Explanation: Deduces separable count estimates and a nonlinear entropy-defect comparison from the alternative covariance bounds.
- **C34** (bound, [source](#stmt-cons-legacy-regular)): **Corollary 34** (Coefficient-three and nonlinear counts). * A loopless labelled multigraph $G$ on $2m>0$ vertices containing $k\ge1$ pairwise edge-disjoint perfect matchings satisfies $$\begin{aligned} N(G)&\ge b_k^m\ge(e^{-3}k)^m,\label{cons:legacy-three-count}\\ N(G)&\ge\bigl[k\exp\{-8(1-1/k)\}\bigr]^m \ge(e^{-8}k)^m.\label{cons:legacy-eight-count} \end{aligned}$$ The same two conclusions hold when $G$ is $k$-regular and every odd cut has at least $k$ edges.*
  - Explanation: Derives structural lower counts from the coefficient-three and nonlinear entropy comparisons under the stated matching or odd-cut hypotheses.
- **C35** (bound, [source](#stmt-cons-legacy-partition)): If the entire edge set of a loopless labelled graph on $2m>0$ vertices is partitioned by $k\ge1$ perfect matchings, then $$N(G)\ge\bigl[k\exp\{-8(1-1/k)\}\bigr]^m\ge(e^{-8}k)^m.$$ When $k=1$ the count is exactly one. If $k=0$ and the order is positive, the graph is edgeless and its count is zero; only the coarse bound is then asserted. On the empty vertex set there is one empty matching.
  - Explanation: Handles the stronger hypothesis that the entire edge set is partitioned into perfect matchings, including its degenerate cases.
- **C36** (bound, [source](#stmt-b-thm-nonlinear)): For every $y\in P(G)$, $$F(y)-H(y)\le 8m\bigl(1-e^{-F(y)/m}\bigr). \label{b:eq-nonlinear}$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the entropy defect by a nonlinear function of marginal entropy on a nonempty matching polytope of positive order.
- **C37** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-c-prop-loops)): There is one deterministic algorithm which, given a graph on $n$ vertices with nonnegative binary integer pair and singleton-loop multiplicities, returns a nonnegative rational number $A_{\mathrm{loop}}$ such that $$\frac{N_{\mathrm{loop}}}{2^{18n}} \le A_{\mathrm{loop}}\le N_{\mathrm{loop}}.$$ It returns zero exactly when $N_{\mathrm{loop}}=0$. Its running time and output length are polynomial in the input bit length, including when the input gives $n$ in binary and lists only the positive pair and loop records.
  - Explanation: Reduces singleton-loop counting to ordinary matching counts and gives a rational approximation with exact zero recognition.

          "exact_quote": "For every graph $G$ as above, $$\\label{geom:eq-polytope}\n P(G)=\\left\\{x\\in\\mathbb R^E:\n \\begin{aligned}\n x_e&\\ge0 &&(e\\in E),\\\\\n x(\\delta(v))&=1 &&(v\\in V),\\\\\n x(\\delta(S))&\\ge1 &&(S\\subseteq V,\\ |S|\\text{ odd})\n \\end{aligned}\\right\\}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-113.2"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-lem-face",
        "exact_quote": "For every $x\\in P(G)$, the space $T(x)$ consists exactly of the vectors satisfying $$\\label{geom:eq-directions}\n\\begin{aligned}\n h_e&=0 &&\\text{if }x_e=0,\\\\\n h(\\delta(v))&=0 &&(v\\in V),\\\\\n h(\\delta(S))&=0\n   &&\\text{if }|S|\\text{ is odd and }x(\\delta(S))=1.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $F_x$ is the minimal face containing $x$, and $$\\label{geom:eq-face-span}\n \\operatorname{aff}F_x=x+T(x),\\qquad\n F_x=\\operatorname{conv}\\mathcal A_x,\\qquad\n T(x)=\\operatorname{span}\\{a-x:a\\in\\mathcal A_x\\}.$$ The point $x$ lies in the relative interior of $F_x$, and every law on perfect matching vectors with mean $x$ is supported on $\\mathcal A_x$.",
        "source_id": "PM-113.2"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-113.2"

        "anchor": "stmt-geom-lem-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $x\\in P(G)$. The number of vertices in $V_f(x)$ is even; write $|V_f(x)|=2m_f$. Let $G_f=(V_f(x),E_f(x))$ and $\\bar x=x|_{E_f(x)}$. The coordinate extension $$\\label{geom:eq-extension}\n \\iota(z)_e=\n \\begin{cases}\n z_e,&e\\in E_f(x),\\\\\n x_e,&e\\notin E_f(x)\n \\end{cases}$$ is an affine isomorphism from $P(G_f)$ onto the face $$Q_x=\\{y\\in P(G):y_e=x_e\\text{ for }e\\notin E_f(x)\\}.$$ It sends the minimal face of $\\bar x$ onto $F_x$. For every $z\\in P(G_f)$, removing the fixed edges gives a bijection between laws with mean $\\iota(z)$ and laws on perfect matchings of $G_f$ with mean $z$, preserving their probabilities. Moreover, $$\\label{geom:eq-fractional-rank}\n \\sum_{e\\in E_f(x)}x_e=m_f,\\qquad\n d(x)\\ge |E_f(x)|-3m_f+2\\quad\\text{if }m_f\\ge1.$$ If $m_f=0$, then $x$ is a matching vector and $d(x)=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-113.2"
      },

For every $z\\in P(G_f)$, removing the fixed edges gives a bijection between laws with mean $\\iota(z)$ and laws on perfect matchings of $G_f$ with mean $z$, preserving their probabilities. Moreover, $$\\label{geom:eq-fractional-rank}\n \\sum_{e\\in E_f(x)}x_e=m_f,\\qquad\n d(x)\\ge |E_f(x)|-3m_f+2\\quad\\text{if }m_f\\ge1.$$ If $m_f=0$, then $x$ is a matching vector and $d(x)=0$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cons-structural",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a finite loopless multigraph on $2m>0$ vertices that contains $k\\ge1$ pairwise edge-disjoint perfect matchings. If $k=1$, then $N(G)\\ge1$.           "anchor": "stmt-d-lem-exact-penalty",
          "exact_quote": "The function $\\Phi$ is concave on $[0,1]^p$, and $$\\max_{x\\in[0,1]^p}\\Phi(x)=f^*.$$ At every point of the box, $\\Phi$ has an integral supergradient $g$ satisfying $\\|g\\|_2^2\\le D_0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-113.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-d-lem-exact-penalty"
    },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-d-lem-projected-ascent",
        "exact_quote": "Let $N_{\\mathrm{it}}=pD_0$, set $x^{(0)}=0$, and for $j=0,\\ldots,N_{\\mathrm{it}}-1$ choose a supergradient $g^{(j)}$ as in Lemma <a href=\"#d:lem-exact-penalty\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"d:lem-exact-penalty\">24</a> and set $$\\label{d:eq-projected-iterations}\nx^{(j+1)}\n=\\Pi_{[0,1]^p}\\left(x^{(j)}+\\frac{g^{(j)}}{D_0}\\right),$$ where $\\Pi_{[0,1]^p}$ clips each coordinate to $[0,1]$.         "source_id": "PM-113.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-a-thm-separable",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a finite loopless labelled multigraph of even order $n=2m$, with a nonempty perfect-matching family. With natural logarithms and continuous endpoint conventions, define $$J_c(x)=F(x)-cB(x)\n   =\\sum_{e\\in E}\\bigl[-x_e\\ln x_e+c(1-x_e)\\ln(1-x_e)\\bigr].$$ For each $c\\in\\{3,4\\}$ and every $x\\in P(G)$, $$\\label{a:eq-separable-bounds}\n J_c(x)\\le H(x)\\le F(x)+B(x).$$ For $n=0$, all displayed quantities are zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-113.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "source_id": "PM-113.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cons-legacy-defect",
        "exact_quote": "For every $x\\in P(G)$ with $|V(G)|=n=2m>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n F(x)-4B(x)&\\le F(x)-3B(x)\\le H(x)\\le F(x)+B(x),\\label{cons:legacy-separable}\\\\\n F(x)-H(x)&\\le\\min\\{F(x),3m\\}\n                  \\le8m\\bigl(1-e^{-F(x)/m}\\bigr).\\label{cons:legacy-nonlinear}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Furthermore, $$\\label{cons:legacy-bin-gap}\n \\ln N(G)\\le\\max_{x\\in P(G)}[F(x)+B(x)]\n       \\le\\ln N(G)+2n\n       \\le\\ln N(G)+\\tfrac52n.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-113.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cons-legacy-defect",

          "source_id": "PM-113.2"
        }

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Perfect matching** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A perfect matching covers each vertex by exactly one edge.
- **Matching family, count and polytope** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Write $\mathcal M(G)$ for the set of perfect matchings, $N(G)=\|\mathcal M(G)\|$, and $$P(G)=\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\{\mathbf1_M:M\in\mathcal M(G)\}\subseteq\mathbb R^{E(G)}$$ for their polytope.
- **Law entropy and marginal entropy functions** ([source](#intro:H)): For a probability law $p$ on the matching family, put $\mathop{\mathrm{Ent}}(p)=-\sum_M p_M\log p_M$, and define $$\begin{aligned} H(x)&=\max\left\{\mathop{\mathrm{Ent}}(p):p\text{ is a probability law on }\mathcal M(G),\ \sum_Mp_M\mathbf1_M=x\right\},\label{intro:H}\\ F(x)&=-\sum_{e\in E(G)}x_e\log x_e,\qquad B(x)=-\sum_{e\in E(G)}(1-x_e)\log(1-x_e). \label{intro:FB} \end{aligned}$$
- **Logarithm and endpoint conventions** ([source](#intro:H)): All unqualified logarithms are natural, and $0\log0=0$.
- **Minimal face and support** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For a graph on $2m\ge2$ vertices and $x\in P(G)$, let $F_x$ be its minimal face, the smallest face of $P(G)$ containing $x$, and let $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}x=\{e:x_e>0\}$.
- **Cut and cut indicator** ([source](#geom:sec-polytope)): For $S\subseteq V$, let $\delta(S)$ be the set of edges with exactly one endpoint in $S$, and put $\chi_S=\mathbf 1_{\delta(S)}$.
- **Two-sided face directions** ([source](#pm-h-9)): For $x\in P(G)$, define $$T(x)=\{h\in\mathbb R^E: x+th\in P(G)\text{ for all sufficiently small }\|t\|\}.$$
- **Face atoms** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Define $$\mathcal A_x=\{a(M):M\in\mathcal M(G),\ a(M)\in F_x\}.$$
- **Fractional edge and vertex sets** ([source](#pm-h-11)): For $x\in P(G)$, set $$E_f(x)=\{e\in E:0<x_e<1\},\qquad V_f(x)=\{v\in V:v\text{ meets an edge of }E_f(x)\}.$$
- **Binary marginal entropy** ([source](#cons:weighted-section)): Put $$h_2(x)=\frac{F(x)}{\ln2}=\sum_{e\in E}-x_e\log_2x_e, \qquad 0\log_2 0=0.$$
- **Weighted partition function and separable optimum** ([source](#cons:weighted-section)): For $\beta\in\mathbb R^E$, define $$\label{cons:weighted-definitions} Z_\beta=\sum_{M\in\mathcal M(G)}2^{\beta\cdot\mathbf1_M}, \qquad q^*=\max_{x\in P} [h_2(x)+\beta\cdot x].$$
- **Dyadic entropy interpolant** ([source](#pm-h-26)): Define $\phi(t)=-t\log_2 t$ on $[0,1]$, with $\phi(0)=0$, and let $\psi$ be its continuous linear interpolant at $$0,\ 2^{-K},\ 2^{-(K-1)},\ \ldots,\ 2^{-1},\ 1.$$
- **Rational surrogate objective** ([source](#d:eq-surrogate-objective)): Define the concave function $f$ on the entire box $[0,1]^p$ by $$\label{d:eq-surrogate-objective} f(x)=\sum_e\psi(x_e)+\beta\cdot x,\qquad f^*=\max_{x\in P}f(x).$$
- **Maximum constraint violation** ([source](#d:sec-optimization)): For $x$ in this box, define $$\label{d:eq-max-violation} w(x)= \max\left\{ 0,\ \max_{i\in V}\bigl\|x(\delta(i))-1\bigr\|,\ \max_{\substack{S\subseteq V\\ \|S\|\ \text{odd}}} \bigl(1-x(\delta(S))\bigr) \right\}.$$
- **Penalty parameters and objective** ([source](#d:eq-penalty-parameters)): Define the positive integers $M_0,D_0$ and the function $\Phi$ by $$\label{d:eq-penalty-parameters} M_0=p(K+L)(2+A_0),\qquad \Phi(x)=f(x)-M_0w(x),\qquad D_0=p(K+L+M_0)^2.$$
- **Box supergradient** ([source](#d:eq-penalty-parameters)): A vector $g$ is a *supergradient* of $\Phi$ at $x$ on the box if $$\Phi(z)\le \Phi(x)+g\cdot(z-x) \qquad\text{for every }z\in[0,1]^p.$$
- **Singleton-loop matching convention** ([source](#c:app-loops)): Suppose a loop is allowed to cover its single incident vertex.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Entropy deficit coefficient | = | $2-\frac2m$ | A loopless multigraph of order $2m\ge2$ with a perfect matching and $x\in P(G)$. |
| Q2 | Positive-coordinate face codimension | ≤ | $3m-2$ | A feasible mean positive on every edge of a loopless multigraph of order $2m\ge2$. |
| Q3 | Entropy defect | ≤ | $2m-2$ | A feasible matching mean on positive even order with a nonempty matching family. |
| Q4 | Binary-coordinate entropy excess | ≤ | $3m-2$ | Maximization over the nonempty perfect-matching polytope on $2m>0$ vertices. |
| Q5 | Perfect-matching count | ≥ | $k^m(1-1/k)^{2(m-1)(k-1)}$ | A loopless multigraph on $2m>0$ vertices containing $k\ge2$ pairwise edge-disjoint perfect matchings. |
| Q6 | Universal entropy comparison coefficient | ≥ | $\frac{\ln3}{2\ln(3/2)}$ | An asserted universal lower comparison on all feasible means; the same necessary bound applies to simple graphs. |
| Q7 | Weighted binary-log additive error | ≤ | $\frac{2m-2}{\ln2}$ | Finite real edge weights on a loopless labelled graph on $2m\ge2$ vertices with a perfect matching. |
| Q8 | Counting approximation factor | ≤ | $512^n$ | Nonnegative binary integer pair multiplicities on a graph on $n$ vertices. |
| Q9 | Singleton-loop counting approximation factor | ≤ | $2^{18n}$ | Nonnegative binary integer pair and singleton-loop multiplicities, including odd order. |
| Q10 | Absent-pair partition contribution | ≤ | $2^{-20n}$ | The weighted complete-pair construction for even $n\ge4$. |
| Q11 | Dyadic entropy approximation error | ≤ | $\frac n2+1$ | A feasible mean in the complete-graph polytope for even $n\ge4$. |
| Q12 | Feasibility repair distance | ≤ | $(2+A_0)w(x)$ | The complete-pair box for even $n\ge4$, with $A_0=10n^2$. |
| Q13 | Squared supergradient norm | ≤ | $D_0$ | A point of the complete-pair box with the stated penalty parameters. |
| Q14 | Constrained surrogate optimum | ≤ | $R+2$ | The stated $N_{\mathrm{it}}=pD_0$ projected iterations, with exact trial objectives. |
| Q15 | Penalty multiplier | ≤ | $1212\,\tau^7$ | Sufficiently large parameter regime in the non-elementary counting branch. |
| Q16 | Gradient bound parameter | ≤ | $1313^2\,\tau^{16}$ | Sufficiently large parameter regime in the non-elementary counting branch. |
| Q17 | Projected iteration count | ≤ | $1313^2\,\tau^{18}$ | Sufficiently large parameter regime in the non-elementary counting branch. |
| Q18 | Graph and arithmetic operation count | = | $O(\tau^{41})$ | Sufficiently large parameter regime after the compact-input coverage test. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-a-lem-tight-cut-gluing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-lem-tight-cut-gluing): Condition on the common crossing label and independently sample the two conditional matching laws. -> A glued feasible mean.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-geom-thm-polytope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-thm-polytope): Induct on graph order using tight odd-cut contraction; analyze incidence-column independence when all nontrivial odd cuts are slack. -> The exact matching-polytope description.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-geom-lem-face) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-lem-face): Preserve active rows in both signs and use zero expected slack to identify face atoms. -> Minimal-face directions and support of all laws at the prescribed mean.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-geom-prop-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-prop-rank): Glue compatible child directions; their cut sums vanish, saving a compatibility condition. -> The sharp positive-coordinate codimension bound.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-geom-lem-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-lem-reduction): Delete fixed matching edges and zero edges, then extend coordinates and laws inversely. -> Fractional face isomorphism and preserved law probabilities.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-ent-exponential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ent-exponential): Use compactness and concavity for continuity, then positivity and row-space stationarity for exponential face laws. -> A positive exponential entropy maximizer on each minimal face.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-ent-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ent-trace): Differentiate finite exponential tilts, cancel the acceleration term by stationarity, and sum over positive covariance eigenvalues. -> The second-variation trace obstruction.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-ent-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ent-trace-2): Combine the rank and trace bounds at a hypothetical positive deficit maximum, then pass to the endpoint coefficient; apply the vertex-star entropy inequality for the upper bound. -> The prescribed-mean entropy bracket.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-cons-entropy-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cons-entropy-count): Evaluate at the uniform matching law and maximize the pointwise comparisons. -> Separable estimates of the logarithm of the total matching count.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-cons-structural) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cons-structural): Evaluate the entropy bracket at the uniform mixture of selected edge-disjoint matchings. -> Structural count lower bounds.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-ex-prop-klee-face) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-prop-klee-face): Iterate the triangle-face isomorphism from the parallel-edge simplex and force uniform law probabilities through tight triangle cuts. -> Sharp face codimension and explicit prescribed-mean entropy.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-cons-weighted) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cons-weighted): Apply the Gibbs variational identity and rescale the entropy defect to binary logarithms. -> A weighted partition-function bracket.
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-d-lem-dyadic-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-d-lem-dyadic-entropy): Complete missing pairs with tiny dyadic weights and interpolate binary entropy with integer slopes. -> A rational surrogate with bounded interpolation error.
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-d-lem-exact-penalty) / [proof](#proof-stmt-d-lem-exact-penalty): Correct degree sums and mix with the uniform mean; dominate the resulting Lipschitz loss by the violation penalty. -> An exact box penalty with integral bounded supergradients.
- **R15** [claim](#stmt-d-lem-odd-cut-oracle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-d-lem-odd-cut-oracle): Compute terminal-pair cuts by unit augmentations, contract even-terminal sides, and lift the minimizing child cut. -> An explicit minimum odd-terminal cut and its active row.
- **R16** [claim](#stmt-d-lem-projected-ascent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-d-lem-projected-ascent): Use integral supergradient steps on a fixed rational grid and telescope squared distances to an optimizer. -> An integer floor estimate with the stated additive error.
- **R17** [claim](#stmt-thm-algorithm-guarantee) / [proof](#proof-stmt-d-lem-odd-cut-oracle-2): Handle elementary cases, optimize the exact penalty, test zero by the partition gap, and clamp the final integer exponent. -> A deterministic lower count estimate and exact zero recognition.
- **R18** [claim](#stmt-d-prop-bit-complexity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-d-prop-bit-complexity): Bound numerical parameter magnitudes, the fixed objective denominator, contracted capacities and explicit output length. -> Polynomial bit complexity.
- **R19** [claim](#stmt-c-prop-loops) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-prop-loops): Adjoin labelled new vertices for each loop count, divide ordinary counts by the factorial lifting multiplicity and sum. -> A rational singleton-loop approximation.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I1**: The unit-augmentation procedure returns a minimum cut separating the prescribed terminals within the total-capacity augmentation bound.
- **I2**: The deterministic counting estimate recognizes zero counts exactly and has an exponential multiplicative guarantee.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A1**: Treat polynomial bit complexity as a practical runtime benchmark. -> The stated complexity guarantee does not establish practical running time.
- **A2**: Use the entropy comparison as a numerical optimizer. -> The analytical comparison does not supply an optimizer.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The integral two-terminal minimum-cut lemma supplies a finite constructive component of the counting algorithm. The executable checks that component; it does not verify the complete counting guarantee.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of symmetric nonnegative integer capacity matrices and distinct terminals.
# PRECONDITIONS: Matrices have zero diagonal; terminals are valid distinct vertex indices.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of minimum-cut sides, capacities, and unit augmentation counts.
# TIME: O(v^2(C+1)) arithmetic operations per instance, C total undirected capacity.
# SPACE: O(v^2) integer cells per instance, excluding input and output batches.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact arbitrary-precision integers via fmpz.
# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise ValueError; output is produced only for valid instances.
import json,sys
from flint import fmpz

def solve(item):
    c,a,b=item['c'],item['a'],item['b']
    v=len(c)
    if not (type(a) is int and type(b) is int and 0<=a<v and 0<=b<v and a!=b):
        raise ValueError('terminals')
    if any(len(row)!=v for row in c): raise ValueError('shape')
    if any(type(c[i][j]) is not int or c[i][j]<0 or c[i][j]!=c[j][i] or (i==j and c[i][j]!=0) for i in range(v) for j in range(v)):
        raise ValueError('capacities')
    cap=[[fmpz(t) for t in row] for row in c]
    flow=[[fmpz(0) for _ in range(v)] for _ in range(v)]
    total=sum(c[i][j] for i in range(v) for j in range(i+1,v))
    aug=0
    for _ in range(total+1):
        parent=[-1]*v
        parent[a]=a
        queue=[a]
        for u in queue:
            for z in range(v):
                if parent[z]<0 and cap[u][z]-flow[u][z]>0:
                    parent[z]=u
                    queue.append(z)
        if parent[b]<0:
            side=[i for i in range(v) if parent[i]>=0]
            value=sum(c[i][j] for i in side for j in range(v) if j not in side)
            return {'side':side,'capacity':value,'augmentations':aug}
        z=b
        while z!=a:
            u=parent[z]
            flow[u][z]+=1
            flow[z][u]-=1
            z=u
        aug+=1
    raise ValueError('augmentation bound')
print(json.dumps([solve(item) for item in json.load(sys.stdin)],sort_keys=True))
```

    "independent_reference": "Exhaustive enumeration of terminal-separating vertex subsets with independent exact crossing sums.",
    "input_contract": "JSON list of matrices c and terminal indices a,b; each c is square, symmetric, has zero diagonal and nonnegative integer entries; a and b are distinct valid indices. Exhaustive tests use orders 2 through 4.",

        "receipt_sha256": "94c0369d2064e1113f8f1bc86d50e968cc91ba967aae89f69022ce198d8843ba"

    ],
    "oracle_code": "import json,sys,itertools\ninstances=json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))\nanswers=json.load(open(sys.argv[2]))\nexpected=json.loads(sys.argv[3])\nif len(answers)!=len(instances) or len(expected)!=len(instances): sys.exit(2)\nfor number,(problem,witness) in enumerate(zip(instances,answers)):\n    weights=problem['c']; source=problem['a']; sink=problem['b']; order=len(weights)\n    free=[node for node in range(order) if node not in (source,sink)]\n    optimum=None\n    for flags in itertools.product((False,True),repeat=len(free)):\n        chosen={source}|{node for node,flag in zip(free,flags) if flag}\n        crossing=sum(weights[left][right] for left in chosen for right in set(range(order))-chosen)\n        optimum=crossing if optimum is None else min(optimum,crossing)\n    vertices=witness['side']\n    if any(type(node) is not int or not 0<=node<order for node in vertices) or len(set(vertices))!=len(vertices): sys.exit(3)\n    subset=set(vertices)\n    if source not in subset or sink in subset: sys.exit(4)\n    certified=sum(weights[left][right] for left in subset for right in set(range(order))-subset)\n    bound=sum(weights[left][right] for left in range(order) for right in range(left))\n    steps=witness['augmentations']\n    if type(steps) is not int or not 0<=steps<=bound or steps!=certified: sys.exit(5)\n    if type(witness['capacity']) is not int or witness['capacity']!=certified or certified!=optimum or expected[number]!=optimum: sys.exit(6)\nsys.exit(0)\n",
    "output_contract": "For each input, a minimum separating side, its exact capacity and the number of unit augmentations.",
    "reason": "The integral two-terminal minimum-cut lemma supplies a finite constructive component of the counting algorithm. The executable checks that component; it does not verify the complete counting guarantee.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of symmetric nonnegative integer capacity matrices and distinct terminals.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Matrices have zero diagonal; terminals are valid distinct vertex indices.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON list of minimum-cut sides, capacities, and unit augmentation counts.\n# TIME: O(v^2(C+1)) arithmetic operations per instance, C total undirected capacity.\n# SPACE: O(v^2) integer cells per instance, excluding input and output batches.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact arbitrary-precision integers via fmpz.\n# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise ValueError; output is produced only for valid instances.\nimport json,sys\nfrom flint import fmpz\n\ndef solve(item):\n    c,a,b=item['c'],item['a'],item['b']\n    v=len(c)\n    if not (type(a) is int and type(b) is int and 0<=a<v and 0<=b<v and a!=b):\n        raise ValueError('terminals')\n    if any(len(row)!=v for row in c): raise ValueError('shape')\n    if any(type(c[i][j]) is not int or c[i][j]<0 or c[i][j]!=c[j][i] or (i==j and c[i][j]!=0) for i in range(v) for j in range(v)):\n        raise ValueError('capacities')\n    cap=[[fmpz(t) for t in row] for row in c]\n    flow=[[fmpz(0) for _ in range(v)] for _ in range(v)]\n    total=sum(c[i][j] for i in range(v) for j in range(i+1,v))\n    aug=0\n    for _ in range(total+1):\n        parent=[-1]*v\n        parent[a]=a\n        queue=[a]\n        for u in queue:\n            for z in range(v):\n                if parent[z]<0 and cap[u][z]-flow[u][z]>0:\n                    parent[z]=u\n                    queue.append(z)\n        if parent[b]<0:\n            side=[i for i in range(v) if parent[i]>=0]\n            value=sum(c[i][j] for i in side for j in range(v) if j not in side)\n            return {'side':side,'capacity':value,'augmentations':aug}\n        z=b\n        while z!=a:\n            u=parent[z]\n            flow[u][z]+=1\n            flow[z][u]-=1\n            z=u\n        aug+=1\n    raise ValueError('augmentation bound')\nprint(json.dumps([solve(item) for item in json.load(sys.stdin)],sort_keys=True))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A counting component can accept compressed pair multiplicities and return a deterministic lower estimate with exact zero recognition. Integration is an inferred use of the stated algorithm. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no game model, simulation transition rule or sampling guarantee is established. Counting matching configurations does not specify how to sample them. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A cut-separation component can return an explicit minimum odd-terminal side for integral undirected capacities. Its row can then evaluate matching constraint violations. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | stated | The paper supplies a uniform deterministic approximate counter with exact zero recognition and polynomial bit complexity for compressed nonnegative integer pair multiplicities. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Contraction blocks and original-pair coordinate arrays provide the bookkeeping needed to lift cut witnesses and reconstruct active rows. The paper does not establish a general-purpose data-structure performance result. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the construction gives exact arithmetic and graph-operation bounds but no accelerator architecture, parallel kernel or hardware performance contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: approximate counting and matching entropy bounds do not establish a cryptographic primitive, adversarial security model or unpredictability guarantee. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: exponential matching laws support the entropy proof, but no training procedure, learned predictor or statistical generalisation guarantee is supplied. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The prescribed-mean entropy bound requires feasible matching marginals in a loopless multigraph with a nonempty perfect-matching family. Blocked use: ['Applying the entropy bound to arbitrary edge probabilities or other covering rules.']
- **L2** (edge_case): The empty vertex set is handled directly; the entropy coefficient involving division by the order parameter is not used. Blocked use: ['Substituting the empty graph into the finite-order coefficient.']
- **L3** (hypothesis): Parallel edges remain distinguished as both marginal coordinates and matching choices. Blocked use: ['Collapsing labelled parallel edges without retaining their multiplicities or coordinate probabilities.']
- **L4** (counterexample_scope): Coefficient one fails for general simple graphs at the exhibited feasible uniform mean. Blocked use: ['Transferring the bipartite coefficient-one entropy comparison to arbitrary nonbipartite graphs.']
- **L5** (counterexample_scope): Sharp face codimension does not establish optimality of the universal entropy coefficient. Blocked use: ['Claiming that geometric sharpness proves the entropy coefficient is optimal.']
- **L6** (hypothesis): The regular-graph structural count needs its odd-cut condition; matching-coveredness alone does not assert feasibility of the uniform mean. Blocked use: ['Using the structural estimate solely because every edge belongs to a perfect matching.']
- **L7** (approximation): The structural lower count may have a base below one at small degree. Blocked use: ['Claiming the displayed structural bound supplies the classical cubic exponential lower bound.']
- **L8** (approximation): The deterministic count is an integer lower estimate with an exponential approximation factor and exact zero recognition. Blocked use: ['Treating the approximation as an exact count or a fully polynomial relative approximation scheme.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): The ordinary counting input uses nonnegative binary integer pair multiplicities. Blocked use: ['Using signed or arbitrary real multiplicities under the ordinary counting guarantee.']
- **L10** (approximation): Polynomial bit complexity is a theoretical guarantee; the stated polynomial has large degree and gives no practical runtime benchmark. Blocked use: ['Using the complexity guarantee as production capacity or latency evidence.']
- **L11** (nonconstructive): The weighted analytic bracket requires a separate numerical optimization construction. Blocked use: ['Treating the entropy comparison itself as a numerical optimizer.']
- **L12** (nonconstructive): Maximum-entropy exponential laws serve the analytic proof; the counting algorithm does not query them. Blocked use: ['Claiming that the counting algorithm constructs or samples the prescribed-mean entropy maximizer.']
- **L13** (hypothesis): The explicit repair and penalty construction is developed on the complete graph with positive even order in its stated regime. Blocked use: ['Applying its repair coefficients to arbitrary incomplete graph polytopes.']
- **L14** (hypothesis): The loop extension uses the convention that a loop covers its single incident vertex and returns a rational approximation. Blocked use: ['Applying the loop guarantee to a different loop-covering convention or imposing the ordinary odd-order rejection.']
- **L15** (edge_case): The nonlinear entropy formula excludes the empty order and has no feasible means when the perfect-matching family is empty. Blocked use: ['Evaluating its order quotient on the empty graph or assigning entropy values to nonexistent feasible means.']
- **L16** (counterexample_scope): The exhibited minimal face can have fewer vertices than the whole matching polytope. Blocked use: ['Equating the minimal face at uniform marginals with the entire matching polytope.']
- **L17** (approximation): The unit-augmentation minimum-cut bound depends on total capacity, rather than only its binary encoding length. Blocked use: ['Claiming a strongly polynomial minimum-cut bound for arbitrary binary capacities from this unit-augmentation lemma.']
- **L18** (hypothesis): The entropy differential argument takes derivatives inside the minimal face after fixed-coordinate reduction. Blocked use: ['Differentiating across different faces or inverting an ambient covariance kernel.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 113 (`lean/docs/113.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A Fully Polynomial Randomized Approximation Scheme for Perfect Matchings in General Graphs](../../preprints/A-Fully-Polynomial-Randomized-Approximation-Scheme-for-Perfect-Matchings-in-General-Graphs-September-23-2026/main.pdf)
> - [Entropy and Face Dimension of the Perfect-Matching Polytope](../../preprints/Entropy-and-Face-Dimension-of-the-Perfect-Matching-Polytope-September-23-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization gives a fully polynomial randomized approximation scheme for counting perfect matchings in every finite simple undirected graph. For rational $0<\varepsilon<1$ and $0<\delta<1/2$, it returns a nonnegative rational estimate with relative error at most $\varepsilon$ with probability at least $1-\delta$. If the graph has no perfect matching, every execution returns zero.
>
> The algorithm is a fixed finite-alphabet randomized machine. Its worst-case running time is polynomial in the binary input length, $\varepsilon^{-1}$, and $\log(\delta^{-1})$, including on unsuccessful random tapes.
>
> For feasible edge marginals $x$ of perfect matchings in a loopless multigraph on $2m$ vertices, let $F(x)=-\sum_e x_e\log x_e$, $B(x)=-\sum_e(1-x_e)\log(1-x_e)$, and let $H(x)$ be the maximum entropy of a matching law with those marginals. The formalization proves $F(x)-(2-2/m)B(x)\le H(x)\le F(x)$ for $m\ge1$, including boundary points of the polytope. It retains the earlier coefficient-eight entropy bound and the deterministic counting approximations with factors $512^n$ for loopless graphs and $2^{18n}$ with singleton loops.
>
> A further statement identifies the exact face obtained by expanding a degree-three vertex into a triangle and shows that minimal faces are carried to minimal faces by the expansion. The paper's sharp global face-dimension bound is not part of that selected statement.

Comparator source `MatchingEntropy.lean` (2107 bytes, sha256 `5e8aacabca23f0b2852ffd7e58c1a87a8e94eec7abb8d5f354d787472d6749db`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

open scoped BigOperators

namespace MatchingEntropy

/-- Nonnegative real weights with total mass one. -/
def probabilitySimplex (Index : Type*) [Fintype Index] : Set (Index → ℝ) :=
  {weights | (∀ index, 0 ≤ weights index) ∧ ∑ index, weights index = 1}

structure LooplessGraph (V E : Type*) where
  left : E → V
  right : E → V
  loopless : ∀ e, left e ≠ right e

variable {V E : Type*} [Fintype V] [Fintype E] [DecidableEq V] [DecidableEq E]

namespace LooplessGraph

def Incident (G : LooplessGraph V E) (v : V) (e : E) : Prop :=
  G.left e = v ∨ G.right e = v

def IsPerfectMatching (G : LooplessGraph V E) (M : Finset E) : Prop :=
  ∀ v, ∃! e, e ∈ M ∧ G.Incident v e

abbrev Matching (G : LooplessGraph V E) := {M : Finset E // G.IsPerfectMatching M}

instance matchingFintype (G : LooplessGraph V E) : Fintype G.Matching :=
  Fintype.ofFinite _

def indicator (G : LooplessGraph V E) (M : G.Matching) (e : E) : ℝ :=
  if e ∈ M.val then 1 else 0

def polytope (G : LooplessGraph V E) : Set (E → ℝ) :=
  convexHull ℝ (Set.range G.indicator)

def mean (G : LooplessGraph V E) (p : G.Matching → ℝ) (e : E) : ℝ :=
  ∑ M, p M * G.indicator M e

def feasibleLaws (G : LooplessGraph V E) (y : E → ℝ) : Set (G.Matching → ℝ) :=
  {p | p ∈ probabilitySimplex G.Matching ∧ G.mean p = y}

end LooplessGraph

def entropy {ι : Type*} [Fintype ι] (p : ι → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ∑ i, Real.negMulLog (p i)

def marginalEntropy (y : E → ℝ) : ℝ := entropy y

def maxMatchingEntropy (G : LooplessGraph V E) (y : E → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  sSup (entropy '' G.feasibleLaws y)

/-- Main entropy comparison, for every marginal in the actual matching polytope. -/
theorem entropy_main (G : LooplessGraph V E) (m : ℕ)
    (hm : 0 < m) (hV : Fintype.card V = 2 * m)
    (hPM : Nonempty G.Matching) (y : E → ℝ) (hy : y ∈ G.polytope) :
    marginalEntropy y - maxMatchingEntropy G y ≤
      8 * (m : ℝ) * (1 - Real.exp (-marginalEntropy y / (m : ℝ))) := by
  sorry

end MatchingEntropy

end

end OAI
```

If the graph has no perfect matching, every execution returns zero.\n\nThe algorithm is a fixed finite-alphabet randomized machine. Its worst-case running time is polynomial in the binary input length, $\\varepsilon^{-1}$, and $\\log(\\delta^{-1})$, including on unsuccessful random tapes.\n\nFor feasible edge marginals $x$ of perfect matchings in a loopless multigraph on $2m$ vertices, let $F(x)=-\\sum_e x_e\\log x_e$, $B(x)=-\\sum_e(1-x_e)\\log(1-x_e)$, and let $H(x)$ be the maximum entropy of a matching law with those marginals. The paper's sharp global face-dimension bound is not part of that selected statement."

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the perfect-matching entropy conjecture of Anari, Oveis Gharan, and Vinzant. For every feasible vector <i>x</i> of perfect-matching edge marginals in a loopless labelled multigraph on $`2m\ge2`$ vertices, the maximum entropy $`H(x)`$ of a matching law with marginals <i>x</i> satisfies

$`\displaystyle F(x)-(2-2/m)B(x)\le H(x)\le F(x),`$

where $`F(x)=-\sum_e x_e\log x_e`$ and $`B(x)=-\sum_e(1-x_e)\log(1-x_e)`$. This bound holds throughout the polytope, including its boundary. We also prove the sharp bound $`|\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nolimits x|-\dim F_x\le3m-2`$, where <i>F</i><sub><i>x</i></sub> is the minimal face of the perfect-matching polytope containing <i>x</i>.
